(6.1)

where we denoted the glueing operation by a double arrow. This enables the computation of objects like projection channel blocks in both two and four dimensions. It is then straightforward to reproduce for example the
n
-point thermal block in two dimensions, given in
[
56
]
, as well as the one-point thermal block in four dimensions obtained in
[
57
]
. More generally, this approach allows for a direct series expansion of
n
-point thermal blocks.

Other ongoing work in four dimensions includes the generalisation to spinning scalar conformal blocks
[
58
]
as well as blocks containing external primary operators with spin.
An exciting prospect to explore is how the vertex operators introduced in
[
23
,
24
,
25
,
26
]
can be accounted for in this framework.

Lastly, it would be very compelling to understand the construction for non-highest weight representations, such as the principal series of
\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{C})
and the induced representation of
\mathfrak{bms}_{3}
or
\mathfrak{bms}_{4}
, which are relevant in the context of celestial and Carrollian CFTs, and explore how this connects to the shadow formalism (
[
59
,
60
,
61
]
, for more recent work see
[
62
,
63
]
). The oscillator formalism for the highest-weight representations of
\mathfrak{bms}_{3}
has been introduced in
[
64
]
.

Appendix A
Unitarity Bounds

We check whether our representation of
\mathfrak{su}(2,2)
on
\mathcal{H}L^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{4})
indeed constitutes a unitary representation or more precisely, we check whether the corresponding representation of the Euclidean conformal algebra satisfies reflection positivity. To do so, we first provide how the generators act on the basis of
\mathcal{H}L^{2}(\mathbb{D}_{4})
.

A.1
Action of Generators on Basis

For the following part, it turns out that it is more convenient to use the normalised basis

\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}=\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\mathbb{\Delta}}\phi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}\,,\qquad(\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m},\varphi_{q_{a}^{\prime},q_{b}^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime},m^{\prime}})=\delta_{j,j^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}\delta_{q_{a},q_{a}^{\prime}}\delta_{q_{b},q_{b}^{\prime}}\,.

(A.1)

A tedious calculation shows how the
\mathfrak{su}(2,2)
generators act on the basis functions
\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}
of the Hilbert space. In fact, said polynomials are eigenfunctions of the dilation operator, i. e.

\mathfrak{D}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}=(2j+2m+\mathbb{\Delta})\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}\,.

(A.2)

The generators
\mathfrak{P}^{\mu}
of spacetime translations act as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{0}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=C_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+C_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

(A.3a)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{-q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+C_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{1}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-iC_{-q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+iC_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

(A.3b)

\displaystyle\quad-iC_{q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+iC_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{2}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=C_{-q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}-C_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

(A.3c)

\displaystyle\quad-C_{q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+C_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}^{3}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-iC_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}-iC_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

(A.3d)

\displaystyle\quad+iC_{-q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+2j+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+iC_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m-1}

The generators
\mathfrak{K}^{\mu}
of special conformal transformations act as
10
10

10

Note that the notation has been clarified compared to
[
49
]
. We changed for example the coefficient
C^{j+1,m+2j+1}_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
to
C^{j+1,m+2j+2}_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{0}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-C_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}-C_{-q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}

(A.4a)

\displaystyle\quad-C_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}-C_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{1}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-iC_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}-iC_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}

(A.4b)

\displaystyle\quad+iC_{q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}+iC_{-q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{2}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-C_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+C_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}

(A.4c)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}-C_{-q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}^{3}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-iC_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}+iC_{-q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+2j+2}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m}

(A.4d)

\displaystyle\quad-iC_{-q_{a},-q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}+iC_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m+1}\varphi_{q_{a}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},q_{b}-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}^{j-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},m+1}

with coefficients

C_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}=\frac{\sqrt{(j+q_{a})(j+q_{b})m(\mathbb{\Delta}+m-2)}}{\sqrt{2j(2j+1)}}\,.

(A.5)

Finally, the non-vanishing components of the tensor
\mathfrak{M}^{\mu\nu}
generating Lorentz transformations act as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{03}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-i(q_{a}+q_{b})\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{12}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=-i(q_{a}-q_{b})\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

(A.6)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{01}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{-i}{2}\Big(\sqrt{(j+q_{a}+1)(j-q_{a})}\varphi_{q_{a}+1,q_{b}}^{j,m}+\sqrt{(j+q_{a})(j-q_{a}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a}-1,q_{b}}^{j,m}

(A.7a)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{(j+q_{b}+1)(j-q_{b})}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}+1}^{j,m}+\sqrt{(j+q_{b})(j-q_{b}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}-1}^{j,m}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{02}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\sqrt{(j+q_{a}+1)(j-q_{a})}\varphi_{q_{a}+1,q_{b}}^{j,m}-\sqrt{(j+q_{a})(j-q_{a}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a}-1,q_{b}}^{j,m}

(A.7b)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{(j+q_{b}+1)(j-q_{b})}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}+1}^{j,m}+\sqrt{(j+q_{b})(j-q_{b}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}-1}^{j,m}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{13}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2}\Big(\sqrt{(j+q_{a}+1)(j-q_{a})}\varphi_{q_{a}+1,q_{b}}^{j,m}-\sqrt{(j+q_{a})(j-q_{a}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a}-1,q_{b}}^{j,m}

(A.7c)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{(j+q_{b}+1)(j-q_{b})}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}+1}^{j,m}-\sqrt{(j+q_{b})(j-q_{b}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}-1}^{j,m}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{23}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}}^{j,m}

\displaystyle=\frac{-i}{2}\Big(\sqrt{(j+q_{a}+1)(j-q_{a})}\varphi_{q_{a}+1,q_{b}}^{j,m}+\sqrt{(j+q_{a})(j-q_{a}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a}-1,q_{b}}^{j,m}

(A.7d)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sqrt{(j+q_{b}+1)(j-q_{b})}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}+1}^{j,m}-\sqrt{(j+q_{b})(j-q_{b}+1)}\varphi_{q_{a},q_{b}-1}^{j,m}\Big)

A.2
Computation of Unitarity Bounds

Using the action of the generators on the basis, we can straightforwardly compute the unitarity bounds through

\displaystyle\|P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\ket{\mathbb{\Delta}}\|

\displaystyle=\bra{\mathbb{\Delta}}\left(P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\right)^{\dagger}P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\ket{\mathbb{\Delta}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{D}_{4}}[\operatorname{d}\!U]_{\mathbb{\Delta}}\bra{\mathbb{\Delta}}K_{\mu_{1}}\cdots K_{\mu_{N}}\ket*{U^\dagger}\bra{U}P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\ket{\mathbb{\Delta}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{D}_{4}}[\operatorname{d}\!U]_{\mathbb{\Delta}}\,\overline{\bra{U}P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\ket{\mathbb{\Delta}}}\bra{U}P_{\mu_{N}}\cdots P_{\mu_{2}}P_{\mu_{1}}\ket{\mathbb{\Delta}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{D}_{4}}[\operatorname{d}\!U]_{\mathbb{\Delta}}\,\overline{\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{N}}^{(w)}\cdots\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{2}}^{(w)}\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{1}}^{(w)}1}\,\,\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{N}}^{(w)}\cdots\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{2}}^{(w)}\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{1}}^{(w)}1

\displaystyle=\left(\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{N}}^{(w)}\cdots\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{2}}^{(w)}\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{1}}^{(w)}1\,,\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{N}}^{(w)}\cdots\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{2}}^{(w)}\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{1}}^{(w)}1\right)

\displaystyle\geq 0\,,

(A.8)

where we used equation (
2.21
) together with equation (
2.19
). Since
\mathbb{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}
we have
\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{i}}^{(\bar{w})}=\overline{\mathfrak{K}_{\mu_{i}}^{(w)}}
. Furthermore, the semi-definiteness in the last step follows from the action of the SCTs on the normalised basis in equation (
A.4
) and
\varphi_{0,0}^{0,0}=1
.

Appendix B
Correspondence with Literature

In this appendix we first provide computational details to the induction of the
n
-point comb block, which we started in equation (
5.12
). Next, a brief review of the conformal four-point block in the case of an internal primary with scaling dimension
\mathbb{\Delta}
and spin
j
is presented. We demonstrate that the result derived through the oscillator formalism in section
5.2
aligns with the one in the literature for the scalar case.

B.1
The
n
-point Comb Block in Two Dimensions

To inductively evaluate (
5.12
), we plug in the result for the
(n-1)
-point comb block according to equation (
5.13
) and obtain for the
n
-point block

\displaystyle G^{h_{1},\dots,h_{n}}_{\mathbb{h}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{h}_{n-3}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})

(B.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{D}}[\operatorname{d}\!^{2}u]\left(\frac{z_{23}}{z_{12}z_{13}}\right)^{h_{1}}z_{n-3,n-2}^{\mathbb{h}_{n-3}-h_{n-2}}(z_{n-3}-\bar{u})^{-\mathbb{h}_{n-3}+h_{n-2}}(z_{n-2}-\bar{u})^{-\mathbb{h}_{n-3}-h_{n-2}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-5}\xi_{i}^{\mathbb{h}_{i}}\left(\xi_{\bar{u}}\right)^{\mathbb{h}_{n-4}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-4}\left(\frac{z_{i,i+2}}{z_{i,i+1}z_{i+1,i+2}}\right)^{h_{i+1}}\!\!\!\psi(z_{n-1},z_{n};u)F_{K}\bigg[\begin{matrix}h_{112},\mathbb{h}_{123},\dots,\mathbb{h}_{n-4,n-3,n-2}\\
2\mathbb{h}_{1},\dots,2\mathbb{h}_{n-4}\end{matrix};\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n-5},\xi_{\bar{u}}\bigg]

with
\mathbb{h}_{ijk}\equiv\mathbb{h}_{i}+\mathbb{h}_{j}-h_{k}
,
h_{ijk}\equiv\mathbb{h}_{i}+h_{j}-h_{k}
and
\xi_{\bar{u}}\equiv\frac{z_{n-4,n-3}(z_{n-2}-\bar{u}_{n-3})}{z_{n-4,n-2}(z_{n-3}-\bar{u}_{n-3})}
. Note that
\xi_{\bar{u}}
is just the
(n-4)
th cross-ratio of the
(n-1)
-point block, where the last point
z_{n-1}
was replaced with an oscillator variable
\bar{u}
. Next, we set
z_{n-1}=0
and take the limit
z_{n-2}\to\infty
.
In this choice,
\xi_{\bar{u}}=\frac{z_{n-4,n-3}}{z_{n-3}-\bar{u}_{n-3}}
and the last three cross-ratios read

\xi_{n-5}=\frac{z_{n-5,n-4}}{z_{n-5,n-3}},\quad\xi_{n-4}=-\frac{z_{n-4,n-3}}{z_{n-3}},\quad\xi_{n-3}=\frac{z_{n}}{z_{n-3}},

(B.2)

whereas the other cross-ratios remain unchanged. Furthermore, we expand the remaining factors that depend on oscillator variables into monomials

\displaystyle G^{h_{1},\dots,h_{n}}_{\mathbb{h}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{h}_{n-3}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n-2}\to\infty,0,z_{n})

(B.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{z_{23}}{z_{12}z_{13}}\right)^{h_{1}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-4}\left(\frac{z_{i,i+2}}{z_{i,i+1}z_{i+1,i+2}}\right)^{h_{i+1}}\!\!\left(\frac{-z_{n-3}}{z_{n-2}^{2}}\right)^{h_{n-2}}(-z_{n})^{-h_{n-1}-h_{n}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-3}\xi_{i}^{\mathbb{h}_{i}}

\displaystyle\cdot\!\!\sum\limits_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n-2}=0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!(-1)^{k_{n-3}+k_{n-2}}\frac{\xi_{1}^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\dots\frac{\xi_{n-4}^{k_{n-4}}}{k_{n-4}!}\binom{-\mathbb{h}_{n-3,n-4,n-2}-k_{n-4}}{k_{n-3}}\binom{-h_{n-3,n,n-1}}{k_{n-2}}(h_{112})_{k_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{(\mathbb{h}_{123})_{k_{1}+k_{2}}\dots(\mathbb{h}_{n-5,n-4,n-3})_{k_{n-5}+k_{n-4}}}{(2\mathbb{h}_{1})_{k_{1}}\dots(2\mathbb{h}_{n-4})_{k_{n-4}}}(\mathbb{h}_{n-4,n-3,n-2})_{k_{n-4}}z_{n-3}^{-k_{n-3}}z_{n}^{k_{n-2}}\cdot\!\int_{\mathbb{D}}[\operatorname{d}\!^{2}u]\bar{u}^{k_{n-3}}u^{k_{n-2}}\,.

We perform the integral making use of orthogonality to terminate the sum over
k_{n-2}
and obtain the expression

\displaystyle\left(\frac{z_{23}}{z_{12}z_{13}}\right)^{h_{1}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-4}\left(\frac{z_{i,i+2}}{z_{i,i+1}z_{i+1,i+2}}\right)^{h_{i+1}}\!\!\left(\frac{-z_{n-3}}{z_{n-2}^{2}}\right)^{h_{n-2}}(-z_{n})^{-h_{n-1}-h_{n}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-3}\xi_{i}^{\mathbb{h}_{i}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum\limits_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n-3}=0}^{\infty}\frac{\xi_{1}^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\dots\frac{\xi_{n-4}^{k_{n-4}}}{k_{n-4}!}\binom{-\mathbb{h}_{n-3,n-4,n-2}-k_{n-4}}{k_{n-3}}\!\binom{-h_{n-3,n,n-1}}{k_{n-3}}(h_{112})_{k_{1}}(\mathbb{h}_{n-4,n-3,n-2})_{k_{n-4}}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{(\mathbb{h}_{123})_{k_{1}+k_{2}}\dots(\mathbb{h}_{n-5,n-4,n-3})_{k_{n-5}+k_{n-4}}}{(2\mathbb{h}_{1})_{k_{1}}\dots(2\mathbb{h}_{n-3})_{k_{n-3}}}\xi_{n-3}^{k_{n-3}}\cdot k_{n-3}!

(B.4)

In the last step, we rearranged powers of points and their differences in a convenient way such that we obtain the cross-ratios from (
B.2
).

Writing the binomial coefficients in terms of Pochhammer symbols and rearranging gives us the final result

\displaystyle G^{h_{1},\dots,h_{n}}_{\mathbb{h}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{h}_{n-3}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n-2}\to\infty,0,z_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{z_{23}}{z_{12}z_{13}}\right)^{h_{1}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-4}\left(\frac{z_{i,i+2}}{z_{i,i+1}z_{i+1,i+2}}\right)^{h_{i+1}}\!\!\left(\frac{-z_{n-3}}{z_{n-2}^{2}}\right)^{h_{n-2}}(-z_{n})^{-h_{n-1}-h_{n}}\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n-3}\xi_{i}^{\mathbb{h}_{i}}

\displaystyle\cdot\sum\limits_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n-3}=0}^{\infty}\frac{\xi_{1}^{k_{1}}}{k_{1}!}\dots\frac{\xi_{n-3}^{k_{n-3}}}{k_{n-3}!}\cdot\frac{(h_{112})_{k_{1}}(\mathbb{h}_{123})_{k_{1}+k_{2}}\dots(\mathbb{h}_{n-4,n-3,n-2})_{k_{n-4}+k_{n-3}}(h_{n-3,n,n-1})_{k_{n-3}}}{(2\mathbb{h}_{1})_{k_{1}}\dots(2\mathbb{h}_{n-3})_{k_{n-3}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{h_{1},\dots,h_{n}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n-2}\to\infty,0,z_{n})\,g^{h_{1},\dots,h_{n}}_{\mathbb{h}_{1},\dots,\mathbb{h}_{n-3}}(z_{1},\dots,z_{n-2}\to\infty,0,z_{n}).

(B.5)

This perfectly matches the result from
[
8
]
.

B.2
The Scalar Four-Point Block in Four Dimensions

The four-point block with spinning inner weight in four dimensions is computed in
[
33
,
34
]
. Let us quickly review the results for comparison. For four points
x_{0},x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}
, there exist two independent cross-ratios
u
and
v
,

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{12}^{2}x_{34}^{2}}{x_{13}^{2}x_{24}^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle=\frac{x_{14}^{2}x_{23}^{2}}{x_{13}^{2}x_{24}^{2}}\,,

(B.6)

where we adapted the usual notation
x_{ij}=\absolutevalue*{x_i-x_j}
. Moreover, we can reparameterise the cross-ratios in terms of new variables
(z,\bar{z})
via
u=z\bar{z}
and
v=(1-z)(1-\bar{z})
. Expressed through these, the four-point block is given by

\displaystyle G^{\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{4}}_{(\mathbb{\Delta},j)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{z-\bar{z}}\left(z^{\lambda_{1}+1}\bar{z}^{\lambda_{2}}{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\lambda_{1}+a,\lambda_{1}+b\\
2\lambda_{1}\end{matrix};z\right){}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\lambda_{2}+a-1,\lambda_{2}+b-1\\
2\lambda_{2}-2\end{matrix};\bar{z}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(z\leftrightarrow\bar{z})\ \Biggl)

(B.7)

with parameters

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lambda_{1}&=\frac{1}{2}(\mathbb{\Delta}+j)\,,\qquad a=\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{1})\,,\\
\lambda_{2}&=\frac{1}{2}(\mathbb{\Delta}-j)\,,\qquad b=\frac{1}{2}(\Delta_{3}-\Delta_{4})\,,\end{split}

(B.8)

where
(\mathbb{\Delta},j_{1}=j,j_{2}=j)
are scaling dimension and spin of the internal primary. Note that the second summand in equation (
B.7
) is necessary to make the block manifestly symmetric in the two variables. In the scalar case
j=0
, one has
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}
and thus the first part of the conformal block reads

\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}z\,\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\Delta}+a,\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\Delta}+b\\
\mathbb{\Delta}\end{matrix};z\right)\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\Delta}+a-1,\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{\Delta}+b-1\\
\mathbb{\Delta}-2\end{matrix};\bar{z}\right)\,.

(B.9)

In the following, we show that our result in equation (
5.23
) actually matches equation (
B.9
).
Setting
k=2j+1
, we express the coefficients of (
5.23
) in terms of Pochhammer symbols as follows

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{N}^{\frac{k-1}{2},m}_{\beta}\right)^{2}\left(\mathcal{N}^{\frac{k-1}{2},m}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}\left(\mathcal{N}^{\frac{k-1}{2},m}_{\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)\,k}{(\beta-1)(\varepsilon-1)}\frac{\binom{m+\beta-2}{\beta-2}\binom{m+k+\beta-2}{\beta-2}\binom{m+\varepsilon-2}{\varepsilon-2}\binom{m+k+\varepsilon-2}{\varepsilon-2}}{\binom{m+\mathbb{\Delta}-2}{\mathbb{\Delta}-2}\binom{m+k+\mathbb{\Delta}-2}{\mathbb{\Delta}-2}}

(B.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{k}{m!(m+k)!}\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}-1}{(\beta-1)(\varepsilon-1)}\frac{(\beta-1)_{m}(\beta-1)_{m+k}(\varepsilon-1)_{m}(\varepsilon-1)_{m+k}}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m}(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m+k}}\,.

In the last step, we used the identity
\binom{a+m}{a}=\frac{(a+1)_{m}}{m!}
.
Next, we expand the first factor and, after some rearranging and using that
(a)_{m-1}=\frac{(a-1)_{m}}{a-1}
, arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{2}&=\left(\frac{1}{(m+k-1)!m!}-\frac{1}{(m-1)!(m+k)!}\right)\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}-1}{(\beta-1)(\varepsilon-1)}\\
&\qquad\cdot\frac{(\beta-1)_{m}(\beta-1)_{m+k}(\varepsilon-1)_{m}(\varepsilon-1)_{m+k}}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m}(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m+k}}\\
&=\frac{1}{(m+k-1)!m!}\frac{(\beta)_{m+k-1}(\varepsilon)_{m+k-1}(\beta-1)_{m}(\varepsilon-1)_{m}}{(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m+k-1}(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m}}\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{(m-1)!(m+k)!}\frac{(\beta)_{m-1}(\varepsilon)_{m-1}(\beta-1)_{m+k}(\varepsilon-1)_{m+k}}{(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m-1}(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m+k}}\,.\end{split}

(B.11)

Inserting helpful zeros of the form
\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m}}-\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m}}
in the first summand and
\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m+k}}-\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m+k}}
in the second summand, (
B.11
) amounts to

\displaystyle=\frac{(\beta)_{m+k-1}(\varepsilon)_{m+k-1}(\beta-1)_{m}(\varepsilon-1)_{m}}{(m+k-1)!m!(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m+k-1}}\left(\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m}}-\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m}}+\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m}}\right)

(B.12)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{(\beta)_{m-1}(\varepsilon)_{m-1}(\beta-1)_{m+k}(\varepsilon-1)_{m+k}}{(m-1)!(m+k)!(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m-1}}\left(\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-1)_{m+k}}-\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m+k}}+\frac{1}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m+k}}\right)\,.

Finally, one arrives at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{2}&=\frac{1}{(m+k-1)!m!}\frac{(\beta)_{m+k-1}(\varepsilon)_{m+k-1}(\beta-1)_{m}(\varepsilon-1)_{m}}{(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m+k-1}(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m}}\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{(m-1)!(m+k)!}\frac{(\beta)_{m-1}(\varepsilon)_{m-1}(\beta-1)_{m+k}(\varepsilon-1)_{m+k}}{(\mathbb{\Delta})_{m-1}(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{m+k}}\\
&=\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{m+k-1;m}-\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{m-1;m+k}\,,\end{split}

(B.13)

where we introduced the coefficients
11
11

11

Note that these coefficients become ill-defined for
a,b=-1
, however, we can simply set those coefficients to zero. In particular, with this convention equation (
B.11
) holds even for
m=0
.

\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{a;b}=\frac{1}{a!}\frac{(\beta)_{a}(\varepsilon)_{a}}{(\mathbb{\Delta})_{a}}\,\frac{1}{b!}\frac{(\beta-1)_{b}(\varepsilon-1)_{b}}{(\mathbb{\Delta}-2)_{b}}\,.

(B.14)

Plugging this result into the sum (
5.23
) and performing elementary index shifts, we find

\displaystyle\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}/2}\left(\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{2}z^{m}\bar{z}^{m}(z^{2j+1}-\bar{z}^{2j+1})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{m+k-1;m}-\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{m-1;m+k}\right)z^{m}\bar{z}^{m}(z^{k}-\bar{z}^{k})

(B.15)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\left(\sum\limits_{b=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{a=b}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{a;b}(z^{a+1}\bar{z}^{b}-z^{b}\bar{z}^{a+1})-\sum\limits_{a=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{b=a+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{a;b}(z^{b}\bar{z}^{a+1}-z^{a+1}\bar{z}^{b})\right)\,.

By rearranging the summations, we can simplify the expression to

\displaystyle\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\sum\limits_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{j\in\mathbb{N}/2}\left(\mathcal{N}^{j,m}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}\right)^{2}z^{m}\bar{z}^{m}(z^{2j+1}-\bar{z}^{2j+1})

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\sum\limits_{a=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{b=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{\beta,\varepsilon;\mathbb{\Delta}}^{a;b}(z^{a+1}\bar{z}^{b}-z^{b}\bar{z}^{a+1})

(B.16)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(z\bar{z})^{\frac{\mathbb{\Delta}}{2}}}{z-\bar{z}}\left(z\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\beta,\varepsilon\\
\mathbb{\Delta}\end{matrix};z\right)\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\begin{matrix}\beta-1,\varepsilon-1\\
\mathbb{\Delta}-2\end{matrix};\bar{z}\right)-(z\leftrightarrow\bar{z})\right)\,,

which concludes the comparison.
